## Workflow decisions integrated from local main

Workflow rows formerly named D067ΓÇôD074 on local main collide with the independent runtime ledger above. Their stable integrated IDs are W001ΓÇôW008 respectively (former `D074` is now `W008`); runtime D067ΓÇôD074 retain their original meanings. Historical main-branch references use the former IDs.

| ID | Decision | Basis / limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| W000 | Extend D066 with recurring schedule and timeout bounds | A daily recurring five-minute trigger has no annual expiration and does not stop a running cycle at midnight. A 90-minute task limit releases the lock after a hung cycle. This changes scheduling, not runtime evidence or quota limits. |
| W001 | Continue the scheduled builder in one project-bound OpenCode Desktop session | The user saw a separate failed `system32` session without the `builder` agent and could not track the scheduled work in one tab. The scheduler itself launches from `.worktrees/opencode-source`; the runner now accepts a validated session ID from an ignored local pin and passes it to `opencode run --session`. It uses the shared OpenCode background service instead of `--standalone`'s private server, so Desktop can receive live session activity. The pin targets the verified project-bound builder session. The old paused architect goal remains separate and must not be run concurrently with this builder branch. See `OPENCODE_GOAL.md`. |
| W002 | Cancel stale system32 work and treat in-progress source edits as continuation | An OpenCode API audit found 15 queued copies of the old Fuzion prompt in `C:\Windows\system32` sessions. Those inbox items were canceled and their sessions archived; no history was deleted, and the project-bound run was untouched. The most recent system32 session was created at 06:42 ET, before the Windows task's worktree WorkingDirectory fix; the only remaining Fuzion Windows task uses the worktree. The runner now gives a short continuation prompt to its pinned session when edits are present and compares the branch head and local worktree fingerprint to distinguish advancing unfinished work from no progress. Only retry/no-progress/interrupted outcomes count as consecutive failures. A scheduled 07:30 cycle resumed the same Desktop session after source-only commit `dff82d2`, with no new system32 session. This avoids repeating discovery and needless Sol escalation while a bounded task is still advancing. See `OPENCODE_GOAL.md`. |

| W003 | Add one lower-frequency independent geometry lane while keeping original execution as the primary milestone | A second isolated source-only worktree at `.worktrees/opencode-geometry` starts from committed `codex/opencode-source` and owns only `src/runtime/nv2a_*`, matching tests and `docs/NV2A_GEOMETRY_CHECKPOINT.md`. Its hourly DeepSeek Go task has a separate lock, session and branch; the original-execution lane remains on its five-minute schedule and must not start geometry component work. Both may use bounded GLM review and Sol escalation under existing rules. No branch merge is automatic; integration requires a concrete original-runtime dependency and review. This increases parallel source work without claiming original rendering or spending Sol on routine coordination. W005 supersedes the original geometry model policy. See `OPENCODE_WORKFLOW.md` and `OPENCODE_GOAL.md`. |

| W004 | Use verified temporary free OpenCode models after a builder allowance or availability failure | Keep `opencode-go/deepseek-v4.1-flash` as the normal builder route. On a recognized provider allowance or model-availability error, the runner retries the same bounded task with `opencode/deepseek-v4.1-flash-free`, then `opencode/glm-5.3-flash-free`, and records the selected model and one-hour Go cooldown. Both free IDs answered live minimal tests. Unrelated errors do not cause a switch. Sol quota leaves consequential decisions open; the documented free GLM review route is explicitly lower-confidence and cannot impersonate Sol. No paid Console balance or global settings are changed. See `OPENCODE_WORKFLOW.md`. |

| W005 | Reserve the hourly geometry builder for DeepSeek V4.1 Flash Free only | The second lane's bounded source-owned geometry tasks do not need to consume OpenCode Go or linked Codex allowance. `tools/Invoke-OpenCodeCycle.ps1 -Lane geometry` now selects `opencode/deepseek-v4.1-flash-free` on every run, with no alternate model candidate or paid/other-model subagent request. A free-route failure leaves edits intact and retries on a later hourly cycle; a material correctness decision is recorded for the primary lane. The primary original-execution lane keeps its Go-first route and existing free fallback. This is a usage policy, not proof of equivalent model quality. |

| W013 | Make GLM-5.3 the permanent architect model and remove Sol from automated rotation | The user's linked Sol usage is exhausted for the week and Sol should be invoked manually only when needed. The hourly architect script now always runs `opencode-go/glm-5.3` first with `opencode/glm-5.3-flash-free` as the availability fallback; the Sol-first candidate and cooldown rotation are removed, and the authorized-architect prompt no longer describes GLM as a stand-in. The architect subagent used for builder escalations is also pinned to GLM-5.3, so no automated path spends Sol. Manual Sol remains available by explicit invocation only, for example `opencode run --auto --agent architect_fallback --model openai/gpt-6.1-sol` in the architect worktree; a manually produced Sol result is still an approved guidance model. No evidence, publication, PR, local-content or playability gate changes. |

| D144 | Model an acknowledging store for the reached PGRAPH interrupt status and clear the recurrence | `src/runtime/device_registers.h/.c` adds a bounded write-1-to-clear declaration (`FF_DEVICE_WRITE_CLEARING`, capacity 4) with `ff_device_registers_add_write_clearing`. A 4-byte store to a declared register stores `value & ~written`, so it acknowledges the bits it carries and the remaining status stays set; the ordered event keeps the value the guest actually stored, and a status bit can no longer be latched by the guest writing it. Sub-dword stores to a declared register return `FF_BOOT_UNSUPPORTED` rather than being partially applied, and a declaration may not overlap a self-clearing register. `tools/fixtures/thread_startup_probe.c` declares exactly one register, `0xFD400100` (`NV_PGRAPH_INTR`), because the reached code reads the status, reads NSOURCE/TRAPPED_ADDR, stores back exactly the observed bit `0x1000` and re-enters its handler from `0x200F59` while the register is nonzero; under plain read-back storage the only writer of that bit was the guest's own acknowledge store, so the model raised the interrupt it then drained forever. The rule is implied by that guest sequence but is not independently verified against hardware; the local xemu-derived PGRAPH reference stores writes as a stub and cannot confirm it, so the model stays limited to declared registers and 4-byte stores. `tests/device_registers_test.c` covers seeded status, single-bit acknowledgement with other bits retained, zero and unset-bit stores, a rejected partial store, and duplicate/unaligned/out-of-window declarations; optimized and ASan/UBSan pass. Private module `recomp-post-memory-build-68` (639,690 bytes, SHA-256 `bd5f5c623f6b3a07dbb02eb0b02c8f62d30cdc54bab7c2520aba04826433f3b3`) reaches 276 distinct routines, 83/83 initializers and 403 kernel operations with `timerCheck = 0`; `0x1FDAC0` is no longer entered, `0x200F59` runs and returns, and the trace is 3,839 events. The new stop is status 10 on an unmodeled read of `0xFD000100` (`NV_PMC_INTR_0`) from the drain/wait loop at `0x1FE4D5`, which tests PGRAPH status at `0x1FE4FD` and PMC bit `0x1000000` at `0x1FE50D`, i.e. it waits for a bit-24 PFIFO interrupt this synthetic profile never raises. A private exploratory profile serving that read as a zero range continues to an unmodeled write at `0xFD002100`; it is recorded only as the next boundary because the register list is at its 64-entry cap and a zero status is not evidence of interrupt-delivery behaviour. Next: model bounded interrupt delivery with evidence or stop explicitly at that read. | D147 | Move the vector alignment guard into a host hook and regenerate the reached translation | The in-macro guard `((address & 15) ? NULL : ff_probe_guest_memory(...))` was eliminated by the optimiser because the generated code immediately dereferences the result (null-dereference is undefined), so the regenerated module came out byte-identical to the unguarded build and the guard had no runtime effect. | D174 | Resolve the three polled PFIFO bits from the fuller retained xemu reference and record that the gate is real consumption | The handoff previously used the extracted NV2A stub, which models no FIFO consumer. The fuller retained reference at .tools/emulation/xemu-source (main project, read read-only) implements PFIFO and resolves the bits: nv2a_regs.h:137-145 defines NV_PFIFO_CACHE1_STATUS bit 0x10 as LOW_MARK, NV_PFIFO_RUNOUT_STATUS bit 0x10 as LOW_MARK, and - correcting the earlier reading - NV_PFIFO_CACHE1_DMA_PUSH bit 0x10 as STATE, with ACCESS at bit 0 and suspended STATUS at 0x1000. pfifo.c:51-52 always returns LOW_MARK for the runout register; pfifo.c:165-173 refuses to pull while CACHE1_STATUS.LOW_MARK is set; pfifo.c:242-245 sets LOW_MARK only after the puller processes a method and pfifo.c:339-341 clears it before dispatching the next; pfifo.c:261-274 and :443-451 stall or suspend the pusher on ACCESS/STATUS and never produce STATE. Consequences: the runout condition is free, the DMA_PUSH clear condition holds because the guest writes only ACCESS, and CACHE1_STATUS.LOW_MARK is the real gate, which the reference sets only after real FIFO consumption - an empty pushbuffer would leave the reference model waiting as well. The reached loop therefore waits for actual consumption of pushed work, which the empty-ring profile deliberately refuses to fake; no producer rule is added and the seam stays unreferenced. Next: the engine workstream must evidence the consumption contract. Sol was not attempted this cycle, so its current availability was not checked. | D360 | Characterize the PFIFO completion flag read-only: fence word, poll condition, polling site and documented writer | Directed read-only characterization with no implementation, hook change, admission, export, seed or translation change. The device context is `0x00203B90`; `[context+0x3F0]` points to `0x03BFF000`, the first dword of the fence buffer, observed value 3 while `[context+0x1C]` (sequence) is 9. `D3D_BlockOnTime` (`0x1FB020..0x1FB106`) polls it at PC `0x1FB0D4` inside the loop `0x1FB0C7..0x1FB0DC`, exiting when `fence >= target` (32-bit wrap) where the target is the routine's second argument. Observed ring: base `0x03B7D000`, end `0x03BFD000`, PUT `0x03B7D7CC`, GET after the first packet `0x03B7D008`. Writer side: `D3D_SetFence` (`0x1FAF80`) and `XMETAL_StartPush` (`0x1FB3B0`) build the fence packet; `CDevice_KickOff` (`0x1FAED0`) submits USER PUT and seeds the word; `FUN_001D060` writes the fence pointer into `context+0x3F0`; the GPU side publishes the completion. The retained executor documents that `BACK_END_WRITE_SEMAPHORE_RELEASE` (`0x1D70`) writes each release into the title's semaphore VA (device+0x30) after consuming everything before it, that XDK D3D's kick marker `0x0310` on subchannel 5 latches `((pushbuffer_address*8 | fence&0x1F) << 2) | wrap&3` into PGRAPH `0x400B10`, and that a semaphore disagreeing with the latch makes the wait last forever (`src/kernel/nv2a_backend.h`, `nv2a_pb_exec.c:3820-3855`, `nv2a_pb_scan.c:245-255`, `xbox_memory_layout.c:1259-1280`). A responder must consume the stream and publish the fence release with a consistent latch; one packet of consumption alone is insufficient. Checks: `test:native` 36/36, PFIFO JS component tests 19/19, `test:runtime` 198/0, `check` 454 files, whitespace clean. Consumer default-off; halt for responder design review. | D366 | Correct the ordinal-159 event gate to decode the full header and record the advance to ordinal 3 | The pinned bridge (`kernel_bridge.c:7646-7648`, `:8342`, `:2021`, `:2056-2057`) recognizes title-built events by decoding the full first header dword: low byte 0 (notification) or 1 (synchronization), Size byte (bits 16-23) == 4; it consumes a signal only for Type 1. The refused object `0x00040000` is therefore a valid notification event, so D364's type-1-only gate and D365's "not a KEVENT" reading are superseded for this object. The pure `ff_vblank_wait_accept` now takes the full header, accepts either type with Size 4 and SignalState 0, and the harness writes SignalState 1 and consumes only for Type 1; native tests cover both types, Size mismatches, low byte >= 2 and signaled refusals. | D387 | Owner directive: name work by feature, not by addresses | The user directed a feature-based naming scheme for every persistent surface. Long-lived branches are `main`, `lane/integration`, `lane/runtime-a` and `lane/runtime-b`; feature work uses short-lived `feature/<functional-slug>` branches deleted in the cycle its PR merges or closes. Commit subjects name functional results (`M<n>: <functional result>` for runtime advances, `ops: <chore>` for orchestration); hex addresses, register names and decision IDs stay in commit bodies, PR Evidence sections and checkpoints, never in subjects, PR titles, branch names or milestone labels - except where the named original routine is itself the readable feature name. PR titles are `[runtime-a] <functional slug>: <summary>` into `lane/integration` and `[integration] <functional slug>: <summary>` into `main`, with the Goal/Changes/Evidence/Checks run/Limits body and a posted review pass for CPU, memory, ABI or GPU code. Task cards carry one functional objective, a human-readable boundary description and one exact-stop evidence line (status + address). Legacy `codex/*` aliases stay frozen and are renamed through the GitHub UI only when no open PR references them. Recorded in `docs/EXECUTION_GOAL_NOV5.md` (Naming scheme, milestone headings, rule R6). |
